561:         if expected_public is not None and published.get("public_key") != expected_public:
562:             raise BindingConflict("published mapped binding contradicts its expected public key")
563:         intents = self._subdir("intents-v1", create=False)
564:         if intents is None:
565:             if require_token_index:
566:                 raise BindingConflict("published mapped binding has no token-index evidence")
567:             return
568:         indexed = self._read_json(intents / f"{_digest(str(intent['token']))}.json")
569:         if require_token_index and indexed != intent:
570:             raise BindingConflict("published mapped binding contradicts its token index")
571:         completion = self._read_json(
572:             intents / f"{_digest(str(intent['token']))}.complete.json"
573:         )
574:         if completion is not None:
575:             complete = dict(intent)
576:             complete["stage"] = "complete"
577:             if completion != complete:
578:                 raise BindingConflict("published mapped binding contradicts its completion receipt")
579: 
580:     def _intent_for(
581:         self, observer: str, *, token: str, provenance: str, key_ref: str | None = None,
582:         expected_public_key: str | None = None,
583:     ) -> dict[str, object]:
584:         intents = self._subdir("intents-v1", create=False)
585:         token_record = (
586:             None if intents is None else self._read_json(intents / f"{_digest(token)}.json")
587:         )
588:         if token_record is not None and (
589:             token_record.get("namespace") != self.namespace
590:             or token_record.get("observer") != observer
591:         ):
592:             raise BindingConflict("mapped binding token already names another intent")
593:         pending_for_token = self._pending_by_token(token)
594:         if pending_for_token is not None:
595:             if token_record is not None and token_record != pending_for_token:
596:                 raise BindingConflict("mapped binding pending slot contradicts its token index")
597:             if (
598:                 pending_for_token.get("namespace") != self.namespace
599:                 or pending_for_token.get("observer") != observer
600:             ):
601:                 raise BindingConflict("mapped binding token already owns another pending slot")
602:         pending_dir = self._subdir("pending-v1", create=False)
603:         slot_pending = (
604:             None
605:             if pending_dir is None
606:             else self._read_json(
607:                 pending_dir / f"{self._slot(self.namespace, observer)}.json"
608:             )
609:         )
610:         completed = self._validated_binding(self.namespace, observer)
611:         if completed is not None:
612:             indexed_slot: dict[str, object] | None = None
613:             if slot_pending is not None:
614:                 # A caller may legitimately retry an already-completed binding
615:                 # under a fresh operation token. The selected published material
616:                 # must still agree with this slot's original durable intent.
617:                 self._assert_published_intent(
618:                     completed, slot_pending, require_token_index=False
619:                 )
620:                 indexed_slot = (
621:                     None
622:                     if intents is None
623:                     else self._read_json(
624:                         intents / f"{_digest(str(slot_pending['token']))}.json"
625:                     )
626:                 )
627:                 if indexed_slot is not None:
628:                     self._assert_published_intent(completed, slot_pending)
629:             if pending_for_token is not None:
630:                 # The pending marker may be the only durable token evidence
631:                 # after interruption between its publication and token-index
632:                 # publication. Never restore that index until the published
633:                 # slot proves it still names this exact intent.
634:                 self._assert_published_intent(
635:                     completed, pending_for_token, require_token_index=False
636:                 )
637:             if (
638:                 (expected_public_key is not None and completed["public_key"] != expected_public_key)
639:                 or (key_ref is not None and completed["key_ref"] != key_ref)
640:                 or (
641:                     token_record is not None
642:                     and (
643:                         token_record.get("provenance") != provenance
644:                         or token_record.get("key_ref") != completed["key_ref"]
645:                     )
646:                 )
647:             ):
648:                 raise BindingConflict("existing mapped binding has a different public key")
649:             bindings = self._subdir("bindings-v1", create=False)
650:             assert bindings is not None
651:             # These writes all precede a completed result. Their contents have
652:             # been checked above, so a retry can now safely make any link whose
653:             # prior fsync was interrupted durable.
654:             _fsync_directory(bindings)
655:             _fsync_directory(self._key_dir(str(completed["key_ref"])))
656:             if slot_pending is not None:
657:                 assert pending_dir is not None
658:                 _fsync_directory(pending_dir)
659:                 if intents is not None and indexed_slot is not None:
660:                     _fsync_directory(intents)
661:             if pending_for_token is not None:
662:                 # The pending marker may be the only durable token evidence
663:                 # after interruption between its publication and token-index
664:                 # publication. Restore that index only after the published
665:                 # slot has passed the retained-intent check above.
666:                 self._ensure_token_index(pending_for_token)
667:                 self._assert_published_intent(completed, pending_for_token)
668:                 self._publish_completion(pending_for_token)
669:             return {
670:                 "completed": True,
671:                 "key_ref": completed["key_ref"],
672:                 "public_key": completed["public_key"],
673:                 "provenance": completed["provenance"],
674:                 "token": token,
675:             }
676:         pending = slot_pending
677:         if pending is not None:
678:             if pending_for_token is not None and pending_for_token != pending:
679:                 raise BindingConflict("mapped binding slot contradicts its token reservation")
680:             if pending.get("token") != token:
681:                 raise BindingRecoveryRequired(
682:                     "a different durable token owns this mapped binding slot"
683:                 )
684:             if (
685:                 pending.get("namespace") != self.namespace
686:                 or pending.get("observer") != observer
687:                 or pending.get("provenance") != provenance
688:                 or (key_ref is not None and pending.get("key_ref") != key_ref)
689:                 or (
690:                     expected_public_key is not None
691:                     and pending.get("expected_public_key") != expected_public_key
692:                 )
693:             ):
694:                 raise BindingConflict("mapped binding token was reused with different intent")
695:             assert pending_dir is not None
696:             _fsync_directory(pending_dir)
697:             self._ensure_token_index(pending)
698:             return pending
699:         intent = self._new_intent(
700:             observer,
701:             token=token,
702:             provenance=provenance,
703:             key_ref=key_ref,
704:             expected_public_key=expected_public_key,
705:         )
706:         pending_dir = self._subdir("pending-v1", create=True)
707:         assert pending_dir is not None
708:         # The slot marker contains the complete intent. If a process dies before
709:         # the token index is linked, callers which know observer/token can still
710:         # recover coherently; no directory scan or candidate deletion occurs.
711:         if not _publish_no_clobber(
712:             pending_dir / f"{self._slot(self.namespace, observer)}.json", _json_bytes(intent)
713:         ):
714:             return self._intent_for(
715:                 observer,
716:                 token=token,
717:                 provenance=provenance,
718:                 key_ref=key_ref,
719:                 expected_public_key=expected_public_key,
720:             )
721:         self._ensure_token_index(intent)
722:         return intent
723: 
724:     def _pending_for_recovery(self, observer: str, token: str) -> dict[str, object]:
725:         pending_dir = self._subdir("pending-v1", create=False)
726:         if pending_dir is None:
727:             raise BindingRecoveryRequired("no durable mapped binding intent exists")
728:         pending = self._read_json(
729:             pending_dir / f"{self._slot(self.namespace, observer)}.json"
730:         )
731:         if pending is None:
732:             raise BindingRecoveryRequired("no durable mapped binding intent exists")
733:         if (
734:             pending.get("schema") != _INTENT_SCHEMA
735:             or pending.get("namespace") != self.namespace
736:             or pending.get("observer") != observer
737:             or pending.get("token") != token
738:             or pending.get("stage") != "pending"
739:             or pending.get("provenance") not in {"created", "legacy-import", "existing-ref"}
740:             or not isinstance(pending.get("key_ref"), str)
741:         ):
742:             raise BindingRecoveryRequired("mapped binding recovery token does not match a pending intent")
743:         if self._pending_by_token(token) != pending:
744:             raise BindingConflict("mapped binding token owns a contradictory pending slot")
745:         self._key_dir(str(pending["key_ref"]))
746:         return pending
747: 
748:     def _hook(self, phase: str, intent: dict[str, object]) -> None:
749:         if self._creation_hook is not None:
750:             self._creation_hook(phase, dict(intent))
751: 
752:     def _ensure_created_key(self, intent: dict[str, object]):
753:         key_ref = str(intent["key_ref"])
754:         keys = self._subdir("keys-v1", create=True)
755:         assert keys is not None
756:         key_dir = keys / key_ref
757:         _directory(key_dir, create=True)
758:         mode = _lstat_kind(key_dir)
759:         created = False
760:         if mode is None:  # defensive: _directory above must establish it
761:             raise FileNotFoundError(key_dir)
762:         if not S_ISDIR(mode):
763:             raise ValueError("mapped custody candidate key path is not a directory")
764:         private = key_dir / "ed25519.key"
765:         public = key_dir / "ed25519.pub"
766:         if _lstat_kind(private) is None:
767:             # This intent exclusively reserved the candidate ref. It is safe
768:             # to resume a crash before private-key publication completed.
769:             if _lstat_kind(public) is not None:
770:                 raise ValueError("incomplete candidate has public evidence without private key")
771:             pair = ed25519.load_or_generate(key_dir)
772:             _fsync_directory(key_dir)
773:             created = True
774:         else:
775:             pair = self._load_keypair(key_ref)
776:             if _lstat_kind(public) is None:
777:                 # A crash between durable private and public publication is
778:                 # resumable only for this retained, provider-owned intent.
779:                 _publish_no_clobber(public, (pair.public_b64 + "\n").encode(), mode=0o644)
780:                 _fsync_directory(key_dir)
781:             pair = self._load_keypair(key_ref, require_public_file=True)
782:         # A prior attempt may have published both key files and then failed to
783:         # fsync this directory. Validate the pair first, then make the retry
784:         # retain those existing entries before returning success.
785:         _fsync_directory(key_dir)
786:         self._hook("key-published", intent)
787:         return pair, created
788: 
789:     def _copy_key(self, source: Path, intent: dict[str, object]):
790:         source_pair = self._load_external_key(source)
791:         keys = self._subdir("keys-v1", create=True)
792:         assert keys is not None
793:         key_dir = keys / str(intent["key_ref"])
794:         _directory(key_dir, create=True)
795:         if _lstat_kind(key_dir / "ed25519.key") is None:
796:             _publish_no_clobber(
797:                 key_dir / "ed25519.key", (source / "ed25519.key").read_bytes()
798:             )
799:             _publish_no_clobber(
800:                 key_dir / "ed25519.pub", (source_pair.public_b64 + "\n").encode(), mode=0o644
801:             )
802:             _fsync_directory(key_dir)
803:             created = True
804:         else:
805:             source_pair = self._load_keypair(
806:                 str(intent["key_ref"]), require_public_file=True
807:             )
808:             created = False
809:         candidate = self._load_keypair(str(intent["key_ref"]), require_public_file=True)
810:         if candidate.public_b64 != source_pair.public_b64:
811:             raise BindingConflict("copied candidate disagrees with the validated source key")
812:         _fsync_directory(key_dir)
813:         self._hook("key-published", intent)
814:         return candidate, created
815: 
816:     def _load_external_key(self, key_dir: Path):
817:         mode = _lstat_kind(key_dir)
818:         if mode is None or not S_ISDIR(mode):
819:             raise FileNotFoundError(key_dir)
820:         private = key_dir / "ed25519.key"
821:         _regular(private)
822:         pair = ed25519.load(key_dir)
823:         public = key_dir / "ed25519.pub"
824:         if _regular(public, required=False) and public.read_text(encoding="utf-8") != pair.public_b64 + "\n":
825:             raise ValueError("source custody public evidence disagrees with private key")
826:         return pair
827: 
828:     def _publish_binding(self, intent: dict[str, object], pair) -> tuple[dict[str, object], bool]:
829:         expected = intent.get("expected_public_key")
830:         if expected is not None and pair.public_b64 != expected:
831:             raise BindingConflict("candidate key does not match expected public key")
832:         record: dict[str, object] = {
833:             "schema": _SCHEMA,
834:             "namespace": self.namespace,
835:             "observer": intent["observer"],
836:             "key_ref": intent["key_ref"],
837:             "algorithm": "ed25519",
838:             "public_key": pair.public_b64,
839:             "provenance": intent["provenance"],
840:         }
841:         bindings = self._subdir("bindings-v1", create=True)
842:         assert bindings is not None
843:         published = _publish_no_clobber(
844:             bindings / f"{self._slot(self.namespace, str(intent['observer']))}.json", _json_bytes(record)
845:         )
846:         if not published:
847:             existing = self._binding_record(self.namespace, str(intent["observer"]))
848:             assert existing is not None
849:             if existing["public_key"] != pair.public_b64:
850:                 raise BindingConflict("another binding won with a different public key")
851:             _fsync_directory(bindings)
852:             return existing, False
853:         self._hook("binding-published", intent)
854:         # Intent files are retained as durable recovery evidence. The marker is
855:         # deliberately not removed automatically, avoiding unsafe cleanup races.
856:         self._publish_completion(intent)
857:         return record, True
858: 
859:     def _mutate(
860:         self,
861:         observer: str,
862:         *,
863:         token: str,
864:         provenance: str,
865:         key_ref: str | None = None,
866:         expected_public_key: str | None = None,
867:         source_key_dir: Path | None = None,
868:         _locked: bool = False,
869:     ) -> BindingCreationResult:
870:         with (nullcontext() if _locked else self._mutation_lock()):
871:             intent = self._intent_for(
872:                 observer,
873:                 token=token,
874:                 provenance=provenance,
875:                 key_ref=key_ref,
876:                 expected_public_key=expected_public_key,
877:             )
878:             if intent.get("completed"):
879:                 return BindingCreationResult(
880:                     namespace=self.namespace,
881:                     observer=observer,
882:                     key_ref=str(intent["key_ref"]),
883:                     public_key=str(intent["public_key"]),
884:                     provenance=str(intent["provenance"]),
885:                     token=token,
886:                     binding_created=False,
887:                     key_created=False,
888:                 )
889:             phase = "intent-published"
890:             try:
891:                 self._hook(phase, intent)
892:                 if source_key_dir is None:
893:                     phase = "key-published"
894:                     pair, key_created = self._ensure_created_key(intent)
895:                 else:
896:                     phase = "key-published"
897:                     pair, key_created = self._copy_key(source_key_dir, intent)
898:                 phase = "binding-published"
899:                 record, binding_created = self._publish_binding(intent, pair)
900:             except BaseException as exc:
901:                 if not isinstance(exc, Exception):
902:                     raise
903:                 raise BindingMutationIncomplete(
904:                     namespace=self.namespace,
905:                     observer=observer,
906:                     token=token,
907:                     key_ref=str(intent["key_ref"]),
908:                     phase=phase,
909:                 ) from exc
910:             return BindingCreationResult(
911:                 namespace=self.namespace,
912:                 observer=observer,
913:                 key_ref=str(record["key_ref"]),
914:                 public_key=str(record["public_key"]),
915:                 provenance=str(record["provenance"]),
916:                 token=token,
917:                 binding_created=binding_created,
918:                 key_created=key_created,
919:             )
920: 
921:     def create_binding(self, observer: str, *, token: str) -> BindingCreationResult:
922:         """Create or recover one explicit mapped key binding."""
923:         return self._mutate(observer, token=token, provenance="created")
924: 
925:     def recover_binding(self, observer: str, *, token: str) -> BindingCreationResult:
926:         """Resume the exact token's durable binding intent without a new key."""
927:         _exact(observer, "observer")
928:         _exact(token, "token")
929:         with self._mutation_lock():
930:             intent = self._pending_for_recovery(observer, token)
931:             phase = "intent-published"
932:             try:
933:                 # A published slot is authority over its candidate. Validate it
934:                 # against the retained pending marker so a missing/moved key can
935:                 # never be regenerated under an already-named reference.
936:                 published = self._binding_record(self.namespace, observer)
937:                 if published is not None:
938:                     self._validated_binding(self.namespace, observer)
939:                     self._assert_published_intent(
940:                         published, intent, require_token_index=False
941:                     )
942:                     pending_dir = self._subdir("pending-v1", create=False)
943:                     assert pending_dir is not None
944:                     bindings = self._subdir("bindings-v1", create=False)
945:                     assert bindings is not None
946:                     _fsync_directory(pending_dir)
947:                     _fsync_directory(bindings)
948:                     _fsync_directory(self._key_dir(str(published["key_ref"])))
949:                     # Restore the token index only after the material-valid
950:                     # slot proves it is the retained intent's exact binding.
951:                     self._ensure_token_index(intent)
952:                     self._assert_published_intent(published, intent)
953:                     phase = "binding-published"
954:                     self._publish_completion(intent)
955:                     return BindingCreationResult(
956:                         namespace=self.namespace,
957:                         observer=observer,
958:                         key_ref=str(published["key_ref"]),
959:                         public_key=str(published["public_key"]),
960:                         provenance=str(published["provenance"]),
961:                         token=str(intent["token"]),
962:                         binding_created=False,
963:                         key_created=False,
964:                     )
965:                 pending_dir = self._subdir("pending-v1", create=False)
966:                 assert pending_dir is not None
967:                 _fsync_directory(pending_dir)
968:                 self._ensure_token_index(intent)
969:                 provenance = str(intent["provenance"])
970:                 if provenance == "created":
971:                     phase = "key-published"
972:                     pair, key_created = self._ensure_created_key(intent)
973:                 else:
974:                     # Import and existing-reference intents have already selected a
975:                     # durable candidate. Recovery never reopens the legacy source
976:                     # or creates a replacement key under the reserved reference.
977:                     key_ref = str(intent["key_ref"])
978:                     try:
979:                         pair = self._load_keypair(key_ref)
980:                     except FileNotFoundError as exc:
981:                         if provenance == "legacy-import":
982:                             raise BindingRecoveryRequired(
983:                                 "legacy import candidate is absent; repeat the explicit import with this token"
984:                             ) from exc
985:                         raise
986:                     expected = intent.get("expected_public_key")
987:                     if expected is not None and pair.public_b64 != expected:
988:                         raise BindingConflict("recovery candidate disagrees with its intent")
989:                     key_dir = self._key_dir(key_ref)
990:                     public = key_dir / "ed25519.pub"
991:                     if _lstat_kind(public) is None:
992:                         if provenance != "legacy-import":
993:                             raise BindingRecoveryRequired(
994:                                 "existing-reference candidate lacks durable public evidence"
995:                             )
996:                         _publish_no_clobber(
997:                             public, (pair.public_b64 + "\n").encode(), mode=0o644
998:                         )
999:                         _fsync_directory(key_dir)
1000:                     pair = self._load_keypair(key_ref, require_public_file=True)
1001:                     # A legacy import can have linked both key files before a
1002:                     # directory fsync was interrupted. Validate the retained
1003:                     # candidate first, then retain that existing directory
1004:                     # before publishing its binding; recovery never needs the
1005:                     # original legacy source for this step.
1006:                     _fsync_directory(key_dir)
1007:                     key_created = False
1008:                 phase = "binding-published"
1009:                 record, binding_created = self._publish_binding(intent, pair)
1010:                 return BindingCreationResult(
1011:                     namespace=self.namespace,
1012:                     observer=observer,
1013:                     key_ref=str(record["key_ref"]),
1014:                     public_key=str(record["public_key"]),
1015:                     provenance=str(record["provenance"]),
1016:                     token=token,
1017:                     binding_created=binding_created,
1018:                     key_created=key_created,
1019:                 )
1020:             except BaseException as exc:
1021:                 if not isinstance(exc, Exception):
1022:                     raise
1023:                 if isinstance(
1024:                     exc, (BindingConflict, BindingRecoveryRequired, FileNotFoundError, ValueError)
1025:                 ):
1026:                     raise
1027:                 raise BindingMutationIncomplete(
1028:                     namespace=self.namespace,
1029:                     observer=observer,
1030:                     token=token,
1031:                     key_ref=str(intent["key_ref"]),
1032:                     phase=phase,
1033:                 ) from exc
1034: 
1035:     def bind_existing_ref(
1036:         self,
1037:         observer: str,
1038:         key_ref: str,
1039:         expected_public_key: str,
1040:         *,
1041:         token: str,
1042:     ) -> BindingCreationResult:
1043:         """Deliberately reuse an already managed key in this namespace slot."""
1044:         _exact(expected_public_key, "expected_public_key")
1045:         with self._mutation_lock():
1046:             self._completed_key_reference(key_ref, expected_public_key)
1047:             return self._mutate(
1048:                 observer,
1049:                 token=token,
1050:                 provenance="existing-ref",
1051:                 key_ref=key_ref,
1052:                 expected_public_key=expected_public_key,
1053:                 source_key_dir=self._key_dir(key_ref),
1054:                 _locked=True,
1055:             )
1056: 
1057:     def import_legacy(
1058:         self, vertex_path: Path | str, observer: str, *, token: str
1059:     ) -> BindingCreationResult:
1060:         """Copy one explicitly selected legacy key after load-only validation."""
1061:         vertex = Path(vertex_path)
1062:         self_observer = _self_observer(vertex)
1063:         if observer == self_observer:
1064:             source = _self_key_dir(vertex)
1065:         else:
1066:             source = _observer_key_dir(keys_dir_for(vertex), observer)
1067:         if source is None:
1068:             raise FileNotFoundError("legacy custody key is absent")
1069:         # Validate before publishing any intent/candidate. A bad legacy .pub is
1070:         # evidence conflict, never something a mapped import repairs.
1071:         pair = self._load_external_key(source)
1072:         return self._mutate(
1073:             observer,
1074:             token=token,
1075:             provenance="legacy-import",
1076:             expected_public_key=pair.public_b64,
1077:             source_key_dir=source,
1078:         )

=== SELECTED DEFINITIONS libs/engine/src/engine/arrival_file_backend.py ===
269: class FileLedger:
270:     """Custody over one ``.arrival`` log, in the contract's vocabulary.
271: 
272:     Satisfies :class:`engine.arrival_contract.ArrivalLedger` in full, and
273:     offers one op beyond it — ``import_prefix``, which the Protocol
274:     deliberately does not declare (see the module docstring).
275: 
276:     **Which verification level each operation invokes** (§06 requires a
277:     backend to say): :meth:`read`, :meth:`scan` and :meth:`head` all reach
278:     their answer through :meth:`engine.arrival.ArrivalLog.walk`, so they
279:     invoke FULL — the log's read path establishes that everything before a
280:     coordinate is intact before it will name what is at it. Only
281:     :meth:`verify` with an :class:`~engine.arrival_contract.Open` scope is
282:     cheap. That is a property of this backend, not of the contract; a backend
283:     with stored head metadata would answer :meth:`head` at OPEN.
284:     """
285: 
286:     def __init__(
287:         self, log: ArrivalLog, *, max_atomic_records: int | None = None
288:     ) -> None:
289:         """``max_atomic_records`` is None for "no configured limit".
290: 
291:         Different from a limit of one: §04 requires every backend to support
292:         one record and lets it advertise more. The file append holds the
293:         whole group under one lock and one fsync with no limit of its own, so
294:         an unset limit is the honest default — but a deployment that wants
295:         one gets a refusal that fires BEFORE any mutation, which is what §04
296:         asks for and what §12's configured-limit gate exercises.
297:         """
298:         self._log = log
299:         self._max_atomic_records = max_atomic_records
300: 
301:     # -- custody -----------------------------------------------------------
302: 
303:     def mint(self, options: Mapping[str, Any]) -> Head:
304:         """Exclusively create one signed genesis and return head 0.
305: 
306:         ``options`` are adapter-interpreted, the same way §02's ``location``
307:         is: this backend forwards them to
308:         :meth:`engine.arrival.ArrivalLog.mint`, which needs an observer, a
309:         signer and the founding public key.
310: 
311:         A second mint raises ``GenesisRefused`` — a backend exception, not a
312:         contract refusal, because the ratified refusal set has no member for
313:         "already minted". Passing it through is the honest option; the
314:         alternative is choosing one of the five and claiming something the
315:         evidence does not support.
316:         """
317:         log = ArrivalLog.mint(self._log.path, **dict(options))
318:         self._log = log
319:         return _head_of(log.genesis())
320: 
321:     def append(
322:         self, expected: Head | None, drafts: Sequence[RecordDraft]
323:     ) -> Commit:
324:         """Compare the full head, then assign and commit — indivisibly (§04).
325: 
326:         The whole transition is :meth:`ArrivalLog.append_marked_many`: the
327:         fence, the full-head compare (F1), coordinate assignment, one write
328:         and one fsync. Nothing is re-implemented here, which is the point —
329:         the append fence is ``flock``-based and per-open-file-description,
330:         and a second appender beside it would be a second way to be wrong.
331: 
332:         ``before`` is derived rather than re-read: the first record's
333:         ``prev`` IS the predecessor's record hash and its ordinal minus one
334:         IS the predecessor's ordinal, so the head the append actually landed
335:         on is known exactly, without a second trip past a log another writer
336:         is free to extend.
337:         """
338:         if not drafts:
339:             raise ValueError(
340:                 "append was handed no drafts — an empty append is a caller "
341:                 "bug, not a no-op to absorb"
342:             )
343:         if (
344:             self._max_atomic_records is not None
345:             and len(drafts) > self._max_atomic_records
346:         ):
347:             raise AtomicLimitExceeded(
348:                 f"{len(drafts)} records exceeds this backend's configured "
349:                 f"atomic limit of {self._max_atomic_records} — refused before "
350:                 "any mutation, because splitting the request across "
351:                 "transactions would turn one logical group into several"
352:             )
353:         entries = [
354:             Entry(
355:                 k=draft.kind,
356:                 body=dict(draft.body),
357:                 observer=draft.observer,
358:                 origin=draft.origin,
359:                 at=draft.authored_at,
360:                 signature=draft.signature,
361:             )
362:             for draft in drafts
363:         ]
364:         try:
365:             records, _mark = self._log.append_marked_many(
366:                 entries, following=expected
367:             )
368:         except StaleHead as exc:
369:             # 1:1, and only from the CAS subclass. The parent AppendRejected
370:             # is also raised for placement faults in a carried-in candidate,
371:             # and mapping those to HeadMismatch would assert something about
372:             # the head that the fault does not say.
373:             raise HeadMismatch(str(exc)) from exc
374: 
375:         first, last = records[0], records[-1]
376:         return Commit(
377:             before=Head(
378:                 lineage=first["lin"],
379:                 ordinal=first["ord"] - 1,
380:                 record_hash=first["prev"],
381:             ),
382:             records=tuple(records),
383:             after=_head_of(last),
384:             durability=_DURABILITY,
385:         )
386: 
387:     def replicate(
388:         self, expected: Head | None, records: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]]
389:     ) -> Commit:
390:         """Insert an exact suffix of pre-coordinated records (§08).
391: 
392:         Composition, not a second appender: the whole transition is
393:         :meth:`engine.arrival.ArrivalLog.append_records`, which is the batched
394:         form of the primitive that has had the right posture all along —
395:         append VALIDATES, it never assigns. Nothing here rehashes, re-signs,
396:         or renumbers, so the records that land are byte-for-byte the records
397:         that were offered, which is the one property replication exists to
398:         keep. The same lock, the same full-head compare-and-swap and the same
399:         single fsync as :meth:`append`, because they are literally the same
400:         code path under a different build.
401: 
402:         Two refusals, and they are different claims:
403: 
404:         * :class:`~engine.arrival_contract.SameHeightFork` — the log already
405:           holds a DIFFERENT record at a height this batch would fill. The two
406:           histories disagree, retrying cannot make them agree, and choosing
407:           between them would be admission into another lineage.
408:         * :class:`~engine.arrival_contract.HeadMismatch` — the head is not
409:           where this batch assumed, either because ``expected`` no longer
410:           describes it or because the batch is not the suffix that follows it.
411:           Re-read and recompute which records are missing.
412: 
413:         A batch whose ordinals disagree with each other, or whose supplied
414:         ``rh`` does not recompute, refuses as this backend's own
415:         ``AppendRejected``/``ArrivalGrammarError``: the ratified refusal set
416:         has no member for "this candidate is malformed", and picking one would
417:         be a verdict the evidence does not support
418:         (``finding:slice2-wp1-refusal-set-gaps``).
419:         """
420:         if not records:
421:             raise ValueError(
422:                 "replicate was handed no records — an empty replication is a "
423:                 "caller bug, not a no-op to absorb"
424:             )
425:         if (
426:             self._max_atomic_records is not None
427:             and len(records) > self._max_atomic_records
428:         ):
429:             raise AtomicLimitExceeded(
430:                 f"{len(records)} records exceeds this backend's configured "
431:                 f"atomic limit of {self._max_atomic_records} — refused before "
432:                 "any mutation, because a partially applied suffix is the one "
433:                 "artifact a replica must never hold"
434:             )
435:         try:
436:             landed, _mark = self._log.append_records(
437:                 [dict(record) for record in records], following=expected
438:             )
439:         except ForkedHeight as exc:
440:             raise SameHeightFork(str(exc)) from exc
441:         except StaleHead as exc:
442:             # 1:1 from the CAS subclass only, for the reason `append` gives:
443:             # the parent AppendRejected also covers placement faults, and
444:             # mapping those to HeadMismatch would assert something about the
445:             # head that the fault does not say.
446:             raise HeadMismatch(str(exc)) from exc
447: 
448:         first, last = landed[0], landed[-1]
449:         return Commit(
450:             before=Head(
451:                 lineage=first["lin"],
452:                 ordinal=first["ord"] - 1,
453:                 record_hash=first["prev"],
454:             ),
455:             records=tuple(landed),
456:             after=_head_of(last),
457:             durability=_DURABILITY,
458:         )
459: 
460:     def export(self, *, through: Head, codec: str) -> ExportedPrefix:
461:         """Re-encode the complete prefix behind a captured head (§08).
462: 
463:         Net-new over :meth:`scan` and
464:         :func:`engine.arrival.encode_record`, and it is deliberately thin:
465:         ``scan`` already refuses a head this log does not hold and already
466:         stops at the captured ordinal, so the export inherits both without
467:         restating either.
468: 
469:         **Streaming.** ``records`` is a generator, so nothing is materialised
470:         and nothing is read until it is drained — the same caveat
471:         :meth:`scan` carries, and the reason an export of a large lineage is
472:         runnable at all. Each element is one record's line INCLUDING its
473:         newline, so ``b"".join(...)`` reproduces the log's own prefix bytes
474:         exactly. The manifest says so rather than leaving it to be discovered.
475: 
476:         **The manifest carries no clock and no host.** Everything in it is
477:         derived from the captured head and the grammar, which is what makes
478:         export → import → export byte-identical rather than
479:         byte-identical-except-for-a-timestamp. ``count`` is
480:         ``through.ordinal + 1`` by density and needs no drain to compute — and
481:         the drain proves it, because a walk that found a gap would have
482:         refused before reaching the head.
483: 
484:         A codec this backend does not have is a caller bug against
485:         :meth:`capabilities`, not a contract refusal — the ratified set has no
486:         member for it, and inventing one would be the over-claim §12 exists to
487:         catch.
488:         """
489:         if codec != EXPORT_CODEC:
490:             raise ValueError(
491:                 f"this backend exports {EXPORT_CODEC!r} and nothing else, but "
492:                 f"{codec!r} was asked for — capabilities().export_codecs is "
493:                 "the list to check against"
494:             )
495: 
496:         def lines() -> Iterator[bytes]:
497:             for record in self.scan(through=through):
498:                 yield (encode_record(dict(record)) + "\n").encode("utf-8")
499: 
500:         return ExportedPrefix(
501:             head=through,
502:             codec=codec,
503:             records=lines(),
504:             manifest={
505:                 "protocol": GRAMMAR_VERSION,
506:                 "codec": codec,
507:                 "framing": _FRAMING,
508:                 "lineage": through.lineage,
509:                 "through_ordinal": through.ordinal,
510:                 "through_record_hash": through.record_hash,
511:                 "count": through.ordinal + 1,
512:             },
513:         )
514: 
515:     def import_prefix(self, prefix: ExportedPrefix) -> Head:
516:         """The §08-default portable import, and nothing beyond it.
517: 
518:         Two targets and no third: a log that does not exist yet, or one whose
519:         every record agrees exactly with the import through its own head. Any
520:         other target REFUSES. There is no merge arm here — content that has to
521:         be re-coordinated to fit is being admitted into another lineage, and
522:         admission is a different operation.
523: 
524:         Returns the target's :class:`~engine.arrival_contract.Head` after the
525:         import, and not a ``Commit``: an import into an empty replica has no
526:         ``before`` to name, and manufacturing one would be the first lie a
527:         replica told about where it came from.
528: 
529:         **The manifest is checked, never trusted.** It travels with the bytes,
530:         so it is exactly as trustworthy as they are; every field is
531:         re-derived from the decoded records and compared. A manifest that
532:         disagrees with its own records is a corrupt export, and reporting the
533:         disagreement is the whole reason to carry one.
534: 
535:         **Replica is not authority** (§08's callout). This method copies
536:         records; it grants nothing. A store that then accepts independent
537:         writes has forked, byte-identical start or not, and no flag here
538:         prevents that — custody does.
539: 
540:         The verified remainder is materialised, because it is handed to one
541:         atomic :meth:`replicate`. That is the honest limit of a MINIMAL
542:         importer: an import too large to hold is a resumable-transfer design,
543:         and this is not one.
544:         """
545:         if prefix.codec != EXPORT_CODEC:
546:             raise ValueError(
547:                 f"this backend imports {EXPORT_CODEC!r} and nothing else, but "
548:                 f"this export claims codec {prefix.codec!r}"
549:             )
550:         imported = _decoded(prefix)
551:         _refuse_disagreeing_manifest(prefix, imported)
552: 
553:         if not self._log.exists():
554:             # A new empty replica. Genesis cannot be appended onto a head that
555:             # is not there, and it must not be re-minted either: a replica
556:             # whose ordinal 0 differed by one byte would be a different
557:             # lineage wearing the same id.
558:             log = ArrivalLog.adopt_genesis(self._log.path, imported[0])
559:             self._log = log
560:             head = _head_of(log.genesis())
561:             if len(imported) == 1:
562:                 return head
563:             return self.replicate(head, imported[1:]).after
564: 
565:         head = self.head()
566:         self._refuse_disagreeing_prefix(imported, head)
567:         remainder = [
568:             record for record in imported if record["ord"] > head.ordinal
569:         ]
570:         if not remainder:
571:             # The import reaches exactly this target's head and agrees with it
572:             # the whole way. Nothing was missing — not a failure, and not a
573:             # no-op to hide. An import that stopped SHORT of the head never
574:             # gets here; it refuses above.
575:             return head
576:         return self.replicate(head, remainder).after
577: 
578:     def _refuse_disagreeing_prefix(
579:         self, imported: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]], head: Head
580:     ) -> None:
581:         """§08's exact-prefix-agreement gate for a non-empty target.
582: 
583:         Over :meth:`scan`, which is the read op that already establishes the
584:         target's prefix is intact — so this adds one comparison and no second
585:         traversal of its own. Position by position against the import, so the
586:         refusal names the ordinal the two disagree at rather than reporting
587:         that they differ somewhere.
588: 
589:         Distinct from the same-height fork :meth:`replicate` refuses: that one
590:         fires on an overlap it was HANDED, this one on the overlap it is about
591:         to SKIP. Both say the histories disagree at a height; skipping an
592:         overlap without checking it is how a replica silently acquires a
593:         prefix it never verified.
594: 
595:         **An import that ends BEFORE the target's head refuses.** §08 asks for
596:         agreement THROUGH the head, and a prefix that stops short structurally
597:         cannot establish that — there is nothing to compare the records above
598:         it against. Reporting success would be a verdict this operation has no
599:         evidence for: it would say "this target agrees with the import through
600:         its head" while never having looked at the heights in between. The
601:         length is therefore checked FIRST and explicitly, rather than left to a
602:         zip that would silently stop at the shorter side.
603:         """
604:         # Dense from 0 (the manifest check establishes both), so the target's
605:         # prefix is exactly this many records and the import must cover it.
606:         overlap = head.ordinal + 1
607:         if len(imported) < overlap:
608:             captured = imported[-1]
609:             raise HeadMismatch(
610:                 f"this import captures head ({captured['lin']}, "
611:                 f"{captured['ord']}, {captured['rh']}), which is behind "
612:                 f"{self._log.path}'s head ({head.lineage}, {head.ordinal}, "
613:                 f"{head.record_hash}) — §08 requires exact prefix agreement "
614:                 "THROUGH the target's head, and an import that stops short "
615:                 "cannot establish it. Export again at or past the target's head"
616:             )
617: 
618:         for mine, theirs in zip(
619:             self.scan(through=head), imported[:overlap], strict=True
620:         ):
621:             if mine["rh"] != theirs["rh"]:
622:                 raise SameHeightFork(
623:                     f"ordinal {mine['ord']} of {self._log.path} holds rh "
624:                     f"{mine['rh']}, but the import holds rh {theirs['rh']} at "
625:                     f"ordinal {theirs['ord']} — this import is not a continuation "
626:                     "of this log's history. Importing into a non-empty target "
627:                     "requires exact prefix agreement through its head"
628:                 )
629: 
630:     # -- reads through the ledger ------------------------------------------
631: 
632:     def head(self, lineage: str | None = None) -> Head:
633:         """The currently complete head.
634: 
635:         Absence and corruption surface as this backend's own
636:         ``GenesisRefused`` / ``ArrivalCorrupt`` — see :meth:`mint` for why
637:         they are not translated. A ``lineage`` that is not this log's IS one
638:         of the ratified refusals: asking a ledger about a lineage it does not
639:         hold is an operation it may not perform.
640:         """
641:         record = self._log.head()
642:         if lineage is not None and record["lin"] != lineage:
643:             raise NotAuthority(
644:                 f"{self._log.path} holds lineage {record['lin']}, not the "
645:                 f"requested {lineage} — this ledger is not that lineage's "
646:                 "authority, and opening a path never makes it one"
647:             )
648:         return _head_of(record)
649: 
650:     def head_at(self, watermark: Watermark) -> Head:
651:         """The full head a projection's watermark names.
652: 
653:         The resolution §07 needs and the query half cannot perform: a
654:         watermark is a coordinate, and only the ledger holds the record whose
655:         hash completes it. It answers or it refuses — a watermark the log
656:         will not vouch for is a disagreement between projection and ledger,
657:         and answering it with a manufactured head would hide exactly that.
658:         """
659:         record = self._log.read(watermark.ordinal)
660:         if record["lin"] != watermark.lineage:
661:             raise NotAuthority(
662:                 f"the projection reports lineage {watermark.lineage}, but "
663:                 f"{self._log.path} holds {record['lin']} — the projection is "
664:                 "not a projection of this log"
665:             )
666:         return _head_of(record)
667: 
668:     def read(self, coordinate: int) -> Mapping[str, Any]:
669:         """The exact record, after establishing its membership in the lineage.
670: 
671:         Membership is not a lookup: the walk that reaches the coordinate has
672:         verified that everything before it chains, because a record whose
673:         predecessors do not chain is not at the coordinate it claims.
674:         """
675:         return self._log.read(coordinate)
676: 
677:     def scan(
678:         self, *, after: int | None = None, through: Head | None = None
679:     ) -> Iterator[Mapping[str, Any]]:
680:         """An ordered, consistent prefix range and no later record (§06).
681: 
682:         ``through`` is a CAPTURED head, and capturing is what makes the range
683:         a snapshot: the record found at that ordinal must be the one the
684:         caller captured, or the prefix it is asking for is not the prefix
685:         this log has. Absence before the captured head is corruption and not
686:         end of stream, so a walk that ends early refuses rather than
687:         returning short.
688: 
689:         **This is a generator, so calling it checks nothing** — the same
690:         caveat :meth:`engine.arrival.ArrivalLog.walk` carries, and for the
691:         same reason. Every check below runs as records are pulled.
692:         """
693:         floor = -1 if after is None else after
694:         reached = through is None
695:         for record in self._log.walk():
696:             if through is not None and record["ord"] == through.ordinal:
697:                 if (
698:                     record["lin"] != through.lineage
699:                     or record["rh"] != through.record_hash
700:                 ):
701:                     raise HeadMismatch(
702:                         f"ordinal {through.ordinal} of {self._log.path} holds "
703:                         f"{record['lin']}/{record['rh']}, not the captured "
704:                         f"{through.lineage}/{through.record_hash} — the "
705:                         "snapshot this scan was asked for is not this log's"
706:                     )
707:                 reached = True
708:             if record["ord"] > floor:
709:                 yield record
710:             if through is not None and record["ord"] >= through.ordinal:
711:                 break
712:         if not reached:
713:             assert through is not None
714:             raise HeadMismatch(
715:                 f"{self._log.path} ends before ordinal {through.ordinal} — "
716:                 "absence before a captured head is corruption, not end of "
717:                 "stream"
718:             )
719: 
720:     def verify(self, scope: VerifyScope) -> Head:
721:         """Verify through a named head; return the head the claim covers.
722: 
723:         **Verification reports; it never repairs.** Nothing here takes the
724:         append lock, and the lock is the only thing that licenses truncating
725:         a torn tail — so no path through this method can rewrite the log it
726:         was asked to judge. (§06 states that as a contract MUST as of
727:         ``decision:design/arrival-slice2-contract-text`` ruling 1; this
728:         adapter held it before the ruling, because the alternative is
729:         reporting an agreement it just manufactured.)
730: 
731:         ``Incremental`` is NOT offered, and :meth:`capabilities` says so. A
732:         verified suffix walk has to start somewhere, and this backend starts
733:         one from a resume mark's byte offset — which a :class:`Head` does not
734:         carry. Finding the checkpoint's position would mean walking to it,
735:         making "incremental" the same work as ``Full`` under a name that
736:         promises less. An honest absent level beats a level that lies about
737:         its cost.
738:         """
739:         if isinstance(scope, Open):
740:             # Genesis and the adopted head record — §06's Open level exactly.
741:             # `_tail_record` and not the public `head()`: `head()` reaches its
742:             # answer through a full walk, which would make this level a Full
743:             # verification wearing an Open label. Reading the tail validates
744:             # the genesis and the head record it adopts, and truncates
745:             # nothing (that lives behind the lock, which this never takes).
746:             self._log.genesis()
747:             return _head_of(self._log._tail_record())
748: 
749:         if isinstance(scope, Incremental):
750:             raise NotSupported(
751:                 "the file adapter offers OPEN and FULL verification only — a "
752:                 "verified suffix walk starts from a resume mark's byte "
753:                 "offset, and a Head does not carry one. capabilities() "
754:                 "reports the two levels it actually has"
755:             )
756: 
757:         if not isinstance(scope, Full):
758:             raise TypeError(f"not a verification scope: {scope!r}")
759: 
760:         # Grammar, density, lineage and the hash chain for the complete named
761:         # prefix. `walk` is consumed through that head, then stopped: Full(H)
762:         # makes no claim about a later concurrently appended or corrupted tail.
763:         found: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None
764:         for record in self._log.walk():
765:             if record["ord"] == scope.through.ordinal:
766:                 found = record
767:                 break
768:         if found is None:
769:             raise HeadMismatch(
770:                 f"{self._log.path} holds no record at ordinal "
771:                 f"{scope.through.ordinal} — the head this verification was "
772:                 "asked about is not in this log"
773:             )
774:         head = _head_of(found)
775:         if head != scope.through:
776:             raise HeadMismatch(
777:                 f"ordinal {scope.through.ordinal} verifies as {head}, not the "
778:                 f"claimed {scope.through}"
779:             )
780:         return head
781: 
782:     def capabilities(self) -> Capabilities:
783:         """What this backend actually offers — no more (§12).
784: 
785:         Deployment tooling refuses an assignment whose required guarantees are
786:         absent, so an over-claim here is worse than a missing feature: it turns
787:         a refusal into a runtime failure.
788: 
789:         ``REPLICA`` joins ``AUTHORITY`` because :meth:`replicate` now exists —
790:         the profile names what a store may be ASKED to do, and an adapter that
791:         can consume pre-coordinated records without assigning any is what makes
792:         the replica role performable. ``ARCHIVE`` stays absent: nothing here
793:         offers a read-only sealed mode, and claiming one would be exactly the
794:         lie this report exists to make catchable.
795: 
796:         The codec tuple grows to what :meth:`export` will actually produce, and
797:         to nothing else. A test cross-checks both claims against the object.
798:         """
799:         return Capabilities(
800:             protocol_version=GRAMMAR_VERSION,
801:             profiles=frozenset({Profile.AUTHORITY, Profile.REPLICA}),
802:             durability=DurabilityProfile.HOST,
803:             verification_levels=frozenset(
804:                 {VerificationLevel.OPEN, VerificationLevel.FULL}
805:             ),
806:             writer_concurrency=(
807:                 "multi-process on one host, advisory flock; LOCAL POSIX "
808:                 "storage only — a store on NFS or another network filesystem "
809:                 "is out of contract"
810:             ),
811:             snapshot=(
812:                 "captured-head prefix; readers take no lock and see only "
813:                 "newline-terminated records, so an append in flight is "
814:                 "invisible rather than partial"
815:             ),
816:             watermark=(
817:                 "the projection reports a (lineage, ordinal) coordinate; the "
818:                 "ledger attests the head at it"
819:             ),
820:             max_atomic_records=self._max_atomic_records,
821:             idempotency_keys=False,
822:             export_codecs=(EXPORT_CODEC,),
823:             limits=(),
824:         )

1498: class FileProjectionMaintenance:
1499:     """Explicit, bounded catch-up for the file adapter's SQLite projection.
1500: 
1501:     Construction performs no I/O. ``catch_up`` validates the requested full
1502:     head against the log before opening a write transaction, then re-reads the
1503:     projection mark under ``BEGIN IMMEDIATE`` so concurrent maintainers
1504:     serialize on the projection itself. Existing rows and identity markers are
1505:     never discarded or replaced.
1506:     """
1507: 
1508:     def __init__(self, log_path: Path | str, index_path: Path | str) -> None:
1509:         self._log = ArrivalLog(log_path)
1510:         self._path = Path(index_path)
1511:         self._closed = False
1512: 
1513:     def capabilities(self) -> MaintenanceCapabilities:
1514:         return MaintenanceCapabilities(catch_up=True, rebuild=False)
1515: 
1516:     @staticmethod
1517:     def _tables(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> set[str]:
1518:         return {
1519:             str(row[0])
1520:             for row in conn.execute(
1521:                 "SELECT name FROM sqlite_schema WHERE type = 'table'"
1522:             )
1523:         }
1524: 
1525:     @staticmethod
1526:     def _read_mark(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> ResumeMark | None:
1527:         values = (
1528:             _meta_get(conn, ARRIVAL_LINEAGE_KEY),
1529:             _meta_get(conn, ARRIVAL_OFFSET_KEY),
1530:             _meta_get(conn, ARRIVAL_ORDINAL_KEY),
1531:         )
1532:         if values == (None, None, None):
1533:             return None
1534:         if any(value is None for value in values):
1535:             raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
1536:                 "projection carries a partial Arrival resume mark"
1537:             )
1538:         lineage, raw_offset, raw_ordinal = values
1539:         assert raw_offset is not None and raw_ordinal is not None
1540:         try:
1541:             offset = int(raw_offset)
1542:             ordinal = int(raw_ordinal)
1543:         except (TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
1544:             raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
1545:                 "projection carries a non-integer Arrival resume mark"
1546:             ) from exc
1547:         if not isinstance(lineage, str) or not lineage or offset <= 0 or ordinal < 0:
1548:             raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
1549:                 "projection carries an invalid Arrival resume mark"
1550:             )
1551:         return ResumeMark(lineage, offset, ordinal)
1552: 
1553:     def _validate_target(self, through: Head) -> None:
1554:         record = self._log.read(through.ordinal)
1555:         if (
1556:             record["lin"] != through.lineage
1557:             or record["ord"] != through.ordinal
1558:             or record["rh"] != through.record_hash
1559:         ):
1560:             raise HeadMismatch(
1561:                 "projection maintenance target is not the full head at its coordinate"
1562:             )
1563: 
1564:     def _validate_existing(
1565:         self, conn: sqlite3.Connection
1566:     ) -> tuple[ResumeMark | None, str | None]:
1567:         required = {"facts", "ticks", "store_meta"}
1568:         tables = self._tables(conn)
1569:         if not tables:
1570:             # sqlite may leave a zero-schema file after a rolled-back first
1571:             # materialization. It contains no projection evidence to recover
1572:             # or overwrite and is equivalent to an absent derived artifact.
1573:             return None, None
1574:         missing = required - tables
1575:         if missing:
1576:             raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
1577:                 f"existing projection lacks required tables: {sorted(missing)}"
1578:             )
1579:         mark = self._read_mark(conn)
1580:         rows_present = has_rows(conn)
1581:         if mark is None and rows_present:
1582:             raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
1583:                 "projection holds rows without an Arrival resume mark"
1584:             )
1585:         if mark is not None:
1586:             anchor = self._log.anchor(mark)
1587:             if anchor is None:
1588:                 raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
1589:                     "projection resume mark is not a verified log boundary"
1590:                 )
1591:             if _meta_get(conn, "coordinate_axis") != "arrival":
1592:                 raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
1593:                     "represented projection does not declare the Arrival coordinate axis"
1594:                 )
1595:         return mark, _meta_get(conn, "own_lineage")
1596: 
1597:     def _validate_identity(self, marker: str | None, lineage: str) -> None:
1598:         if marker is not None and marker != lineage:
1599:             raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
1600:                 f"projection own_lineage {marker!r} differs from log lineage {lineage!r}"
1601:             )
1602: 
1603:     def _insert_record(self, conn: sqlite3.Connection, record: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
1604:         for sequence, (kind, row) in enumerate(rows_of_record(record)):
1605:             try:
1606:                 conn.execute(
1607:                     FACT_INSERT_SQL if kind == "fact" else TICK_INSERT_SQL,
1608:                     (*row, record["ord"], sequence),
1609:                 )
1610:             except Exception as exc:
1611:                 raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
1612:                     f"projection refuses {kind} {row[0]!r} from ordinal "
1613:                     f"{record['ord']}; existing state is not a consumable prefix"
1614:                 ) from exc
1615: 
1616:     def catch_up(self, through: Head) -> MaintenanceAdvance:
1617:         if self._closed:
1618:             raise RuntimeError("projection maintenance handle is closed")
1619:         self._validate_target(through)
1620: 
1621:         existed = self._path.exists()
1622:         self._path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
1623:         # A competing maintainer owns a normal serialization turn, not an
1624:         # immediate failure. Keep the wait finite so operational lock loss is
1625:         # still reported through ProjectionSyncError with observed evidence.
1626:         conn = sqlite3.connect(str(self._path), timeout=30.0)
1627:         conn.isolation_level = None
1628:         try:
1629:             if existed:
1630:                 mark, marker = self._validate_existing(conn)
1631:                 self._validate_identity(marker, through.lineage)
1632:             else:
1633:                 mark, marker = None, None
1634: 
1635:             # The shared schema helper owns its commits, so schema setup must
1636:             # complete before the explicit row/watermark transaction begins.
1637:             # It is idempotent derived structure; projection content and its
1638:             # resume mark remain one atomic transaction below.
1639:             _ensure_index_schema(conn, self._log)
1640:             conn.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
1641:             try:
1642:                 # Revalidate all evidence after acquiring the adapter's write
1643:                 # lock. Another maintainer may have advanced or exposed a
1644:                 # conflict between the initial observation and this turn.
1645:                 mark, marker = self._validate_existing(conn)
1646:                 self._validate_identity(marker, through.lineage)
1647:                 before = (
1648:                     None
1649:                     if mark is None
1650:                     else Watermark(mark.arrival_lineage, mark.arrival_ordinal)
1651:                 )
1652: 
1653:                 changed = False
1654:                 last_mark = mark
1655:                 if mark is None or mark.arrival_ordinal < through.ordinal:
1656:                     resumed, records = self._log.walk_marked(mark)
1657:                     if mark is not None and resumed == 0:
1658:                         raise ArrivalCanonicalUnsupported(
1659:                             "projection resume mark was refused during verified traversal"
1660:                         )
1661:                     for record, record_mark in records:
1662:                         if record["ord"] > through.ordinal:
1663:                             break
1664:                         if (
1665:                             record["ord"] == through.ordinal
1666:                             and record["rh"] != through.record_hash
1667:                         ):
1668:                             raise HeadMismatch(
1669:                                 "log changed at the maintenance target after full "
1670:                                 "verification"
1671:                             )
1672:                         self._insert_record(conn, record)
1673:                         last_mark = record_mark
1674:                         changed = True
1675:                     if last_mark is None or last_mark.arrival_ordinal < through.ordinal:
1676:                         raise HeadMismatch(
1677:                             "verified traversal ended before the maintenance target"
1678:                         )
1679: 
1680:                 if marker is None:
1681:                     licensed = licensed_own_lineage(conn, through.lineage)
1682:                     if licensed is not None:
1683:                         _meta_set(conn, "own_lineage", licensed)
1684:                         changed = True
1685:                 if last_mark is None:
1686:                     raise HeadMismatch("maintenance established no projection mark")
1687:                 if changed or mark != last_mark:
1688:                     _stamp_mark(conn, last_mark)
1689:                 conn.commit()
1690:             except BaseException:
1691:                 conn.rollback()
1692:                 raise
1693: 
1694:             after = Watermark(last_mark.arrival_lineage, last_mark.arrival_ordinal)
1695:             return MaintenanceAdvance(before=before, after=after, changed=changed)
1696:         finally:
1697:             conn.close()
1698: 
1699:     def close(self) -> None:
1700:         self._closed = True

282: @contextmanager
283: def _declaration_lock(target: Path):
284:     """Serialize apply/recovery while releasing the lock on process death."""
285:     lock_path = _declaration_lock_path(target)
286:     lock_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
287:     with lock_path.open("a+b") as lock_fh:
288:         fcntl.flock(lock_fh.fileno(), fcntl.LOCK_EX)
289:         try:
290:             yield
291:         finally:
292:             fcntl.flock(lock_fh.fileno(), fcntl.LOCK_UN)

364: def _write_exclusive_json(path: Path, data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:
365:     path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
366:     encoded = json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"))
367:     fd = os.open(path, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL, 0o600)
368:     try:
369:         with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
370:             stream.write(encoded + "\n")
371:             stream.flush()
372:             os.fsync(stream.fileno())
373:         _fsync_parent(path)
374:     except BaseException:
375:         with suppress(FileNotFoundError):
376:             path.unlink()
377:         raise

380: def _replace_json(path: Path, data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:
381:     encoded = json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"))
382:     fd, raw = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix=f".{path.name}.", dir=path.parent)
383:     temp = Path(raw)
384:     try:
385:         with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as stream:
386:             stream.write(encoded + "\n")
387:             stream.flush()
388:             os.fsync(stream.fileno())
389:         temp.replace(path)
390:         _fsync_parent(path)
391:     finally:
392:         with suppress(FileNotFoundError):
393:             temp.unlink()

416: def _drafts_from_exact(raw: Any, expected_exact: str) -> tuple[RecordDraft, ...]:

418:         raise DeclarationApplyError("declaration intent drafts are not a list")

=== SELECTED DEFINITIONS libs/engine/src/engine/arrival.py ===
640: def content_commitment(
641:     k: str, at: float, observer: str, origin: str, body: dict
642: ) -> str:
643:     """The digest a signature covers: sha256 over JCS of the five content
644:     fields.
645: 
646:     Content only. The coordinate, ``prev``, ``rh``, and ``sig`` itself are
647:     all outside it — the first three because they are custody context rather
648:     than authored content, the last because a signature cannot sign itself.
649:     That exclusion is what makes a signature transport-stable: any store
650:     holding the record re-derives the same digest and verifies authorship
651:     against the observer registry without trusting the sender.
652:     """
653:     envelope = {"k": k, "at": at, "observer": observer, "origin": origin, "body": body}
654:     return hashlib.sha256(_canonical_bytes(envelope)).hexdigest()

1879: @dataclass(frozen=True)
1880: class KeyRegistry:
1881:     """One log's key history: who may speak, with which key, from where on.
1882: 
1883:     The registry a log says about ITSELF — extracted from the log alone, the
1884:     same exclusivity claim :func:`verify_authorship` makes. It answers one
1885:     question, :meth:`keys_valid_at`, and the answer is the ruled slice-A
1886:     placement clause verbatim (decision:design/arrival-sliceA-authority): a
1887:     key is valid at position N iff it was introduced at a position < N.
1888:     Genesis self-certification is NOT expressible here on purpose — it is a
1889:     property of one record verifying against its own body, which the walk
1890:     that builds the registry settles before any registry exists.
1891: 
1892:     Built by :func:`key_registry`, whose walk is SELECTIVE: the registry is
1893:     trustworthy because every record that FORMED it was signature-verified,
1894:     and it says nothing at all about records that did not form it.
1895:     """
1896: 
1897:     lineage: str
1898:     introductions: dict[str, tuple[tuple[str, int], ...]]
1899:     """observer -> ``(key, introduced_ordinal)`` in introduction order."""
1900: 
1901:     def keys_valid_at(self, observer: str, position: int) -> tuple[tuple[str, int], ...]:
1902:         """The keys this log makes valid for ``observer`` at ``position``.
1903: 
1904:         ``(key, introduced_ordinal)`` in introduction order, possibly empty.
1905:         Keys are bound to observers, never free-floating: asking on behalf of
1906:         the wrong observer returns nothing rather than someone else's key.
1907:         """
1908:         return _keys_valid_at(self.introductions, observer, position)

1960: def key_registry_from_records(
1961:     records: Iterable[Mapping[str, object]], verify: Verify
1962: ) -> tuple[KeyRegistry, tuple[KeyResolution, ...]]:
1963:     """Build selective key history from a structurally verified record stream.
1964: 
1965:     ``records`` must be the complete, ordered genesis-through-H prefix from a
1966:     structural verifier such as :meth:`ArrivalLog.walk` or an attested
1967:     backend scan. This function deliberately does not decode, hash, or check
1968:     ordinal/lineage/chain structure again; its claim is authorship over that
1969:     already-verified sequence. Only the genesis and key-introduction envelope
1970:     signatures are checked, matching :func:`key_registry`. The returned rows
1971:     are the verified registry-forming signatures, alongside the registry.
1972:     """
1973:     return _walk_authority_records(records, verify, verify_ordinary=False)

2049: def _walk_authority_records(
2050:     records: Iterable[Mapping[str, object]],
2051:     verify: Verify,
2052:     *,
2053:     verify_ordinary: bool,
2054: ) -> tuple[KeyRegistry, tuple[KeyResolution, ...]]:
2055:     """Shared authorship walk over a complete, structurally verified stream.
2056: 
2057:     The caller owns structural verification. Keeping this separate from the
2058:     file walk lets an authority adapter provide its already-attested records
2059:     without importing or reopening :class:`ArrivalLog`, while the boolean
2060:     retains the selective admission and whole-envelope verification widths.
2061:     """
2062:     # observer -> [(key, introduced_ordinal)] in introduction order.
2063:     registry: dict[str, list[tuple[str, int]]] = {}
2064:     rows: list[KeyResolution] = []
2065:     lineage = ""
2066: 
2067:     for record in records:
2068:         ordinal = record["ord"]
2069:         # The walk holds every record to the genesis's lineage, so this is
2070:         # one value for the whole loop; re-read per record for simplicity.
2071:         lineage = record["lin"]
2072: 
2073:         if ordinal == 0:
2074:             # Genesis: self-certifying, and only here. The walk already held
2075:             # it to every structural genesis rule, key shape included.
2076:             key = record["body"]["key"]
2077:             digest = _commitment_of(record)
2078:             if not verify(key, record[_SIG], digest):
2079:                 raise AuthorshipUnverified(
2080:                     "genesis signature does not verify against the founding "
2081:                     "key its own body carries — the log is not "
2082:                     "self-certifying",
2083:                     0,
2084:                 )
2085:             registry[record["observer"]] = [(key, 0)]
2086:             rows.append(
2087:                 KeyResolution(
2088:                     ordinal=0,
2089:                     observer=record["observer"],
2090:                     key=key,
2091:                     introduced_lineage=record["lin"],
2092:                     introduced_ordinal=0,
2093:                 )
2094:             )
2095:             continue
2096: 
2097:         forming = record["k"] == KEY_INTRODUCTION_KIND
2098:         if _SIG in record and (verify_ordinary or forming):
2099:             rows.append(_resolve(record, registry, lineage, verify))
2100:         if forming:
2101:             # The signature just verified under an already-valid key (the
2102:             # placement rules make an unsigned introduction unspellable, and
2103:             # a key introduction is registry-forming in BOTH widths of this
2104:             # walk), so the named key joins the registry at THIS ordinal:
2105:             # valid strictly after it, per the ruled clause.
2106:             named = record["body"]["observer"]
2107:             registry.setdefault(named, []).append((record["body"]["key"], ordinal))
2108: 
2109:     return (
2110:         KeyRegistry(
2111:             lineage=lineage,
2112:             introductions={
2113:                 observer: tuple(entries) for observer, entries in registry.items()
2114:             },
2115:         ),
2116:         tuple(rows),
2117:     )

103:     ends "Replica is not authority" — a byte-identical copy that accepts
104:     independent writes has forked.

157: @dataclass(frozen=True)
158: class Head:
159:     """The complete coordinate of a lineage's newest record.
160: 
161:     All three fields, always. The ordinal alone is what the pre-slice-2 CAS
162:     compared, and an ordinal-only pin cannot tell "nothing has changed" from
163:     "the record at this ordinal was replaced" — the rollback and same-height
164:     fork §11 asks backends to detect. A head that omits the hash is not a
165:     weaker head, it is a different and much smaller claim.
166:     """

168:     lineage: str
169:     ordinal: int

406: @dataclass(frozen=True)
407: class RecordDraft:
408:     """One record a caller wants appended, minus its coordinate.
409:

411:     ledger's to assign inside the fence (§04 step 5), so a draft names only
412:     what its author owns. ``signature``, when present, is over the authored
413:     content and not over the coordinate, which is what makes it something a
414:     caller can produce before knowing where the record will land.
415:     """

475:

477:     different from a limit of one: §04 requires every backend to support one
