544: @dataclass(frozen=True)
545: class StoreDescriptor:
546:     """Which adapter opens which artifact, stated rather than sniffed.
547: 
548:     Backend selection MUST be explicit: nothing infers authority, canonical
549:     format, or migration behaviour from a ``.arrival``, ``.duckdb`` or ``.db``
550:     suffix. ``location`` is a ``str`` and not a ``Path`` on purpose — it is
551:     interpreted only by the named adapter and may be a filesystem path, a DSN
552:     reference, or a service URL.
553: 
554:     ``lineage``, when already known, is checked against genesis rather than
555:     trusted as a replacement for it. ``role`` expresses operator intent; the
556:     backend still verifies it can safely perform what is asked. Secrets are
557:     referenced through ``witness``/a secret provider, never embedded.
558: 
559:     Locally this descriptor dissolves into the ``.vertex`` store clause
560:     (backend-contract.html:140-149) — WP4 builds that resolution.
561:     """
562: 
563:     backend: str
564:     location: str
565:     lineage: str | None = None
566:     role: Profile | None = None
567:     query: str | None = None
568:     witness: str | None = None

590: @runtime_checkable
591: class ArrivalLedger(Protocol):
592:     """Custody: everything that can change what the lineage holds.
593: 
594:     ``runtime_checkable`` so a conformance test can ask whether an adapter
595:     satisfies the surface. That check is by method NAME only — Python's
596:     protocol runtime check cannot judge signatures — so it is a location
597:     claim ("this adapter offers these ops"), never a conformance verdict.
598:     The vectors are what judge behaviour.
599:     """
600: 
601:     def mint(self, options: Mapping[str, Any]) -> Head:
602:         """Exclusively create one signed genesis and return head 0."""
603:         ...
604: 
605:     def head(self, lineage: str | None = None) -> Head:
606:         """The currently complete head, or a typed absence/corruption refusal."""
607:         ...
608: 
609:     def head_at(self, watermark: Watermark) -> Head:
610:         """Resolve a projection's watermark into the verified head it names.
611: 
612:         §07 asks a projection to store a verified ``projected_through`` head,
613:         and a projection that holds a coordinate cannot produce one: only
614:         custody holds the record whose hash completes it. So the resolution
615:         is a ledger op, and the return type is what states the custody/reads
616:         separation rather than prose. It answers or it refuses — a watermark
617:         the ledger will not vouch for IS the projection/ledger disagreement,
618:         and a manufactured head would hide exactly the thing being asked
619:         about.
620: 
621:         A read, not a mutation: deliberately absent from
622:         :data:`LEDGER_MUTATIONS`.
623:         """
624:         ...
625: 
626:     def append(self, expected: Head | None, drafts: Sequence[RecordDraft]) -> Commit:
627:         """Compare the full head, then assign and commit, indivisibly (§04)."""
628:         ...
629: 
630:     def replicate(
631:         self, expected: Head | None, records: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]]
632:     ) -> Commit:
633:         """Append exact pre-coordinated records; never rehash, never re-sign."""
634:         ...
635: 
636:     def read(self, coordinate: int) -> Mapping[str, Any]:
637:         """The exact record, after establishing its membership in the lineage."""
638:         ...
639: 
640:     def scan(
641:         self, *, after: int | None = None, through: Head | None = None
642:     ) -> Iterator[Mapping[str, Any]]:
643:         """An ordered, consistent prefix range and no later record (§06)."""
644:         ...
645: 
646:     def verify(self, scope: VerifyScope) -> Head:
647:         """Verify through a named head; return the head the claim covers.
648: 
649:         **Verification never repairs**, at any level, and §06 states it as a
650:         MUST: no catch-up, no truncation, no rebuild of existing projection
651:         state, no head re-stamp. An operation that repairs on the way to
652:         reading has destroyed the evidence it was asked to judge and can
653:         only report an agreement it just manufactured, so a backend whose
654:         ordinary open path recovers MUST offer verification a route that
655:         does not.
656: 
657:         The one carve-out: materializing a projection that does not yet
658:         exist is not repair — it destroys no prior state and can hide no
659:         divergence (``decision:design/arrival-slice2-contract-text``,
660:         ruling 1).
661:         """
662:         ...
663: 
664:     def export(self, *, through: Head, codec: str) -> ExportedPrefix:
665:         """Deterministic portable records plus a manifest, for a captured prefix."""
666:         ...
667: 
668:     def capabilities(self) -> Capabilities:
669:         """Profiles, limits, concurrency scope, durability, codecs, query behaviour."""
670:         ...

756: class ContractRefusal(Exception):
757:     """Root of the backend-neutral refusals.
758: 
759:     Rooted here rather than in any backend's family so a caller can catch a
760:     refusal without knowing which adapter produced it.
761:     """

=== SELECTED DEFINITIONS libs/engine/src/engine/arrival_head_seam.py ===
238: class NotWitnessed(Exception):
239:     """The mutation COMMITTED, and the journal write for it failed.
240: 
241:     §05's ``committed`` versus ``witnessed`` distinction, and §D.4's rule that
242:     a journal write failure is surfaced rather than swallowed: the append has
243:     already landed, so the store has advanced past its witness and the caller
244:     is told which of the two it got instead of having both collapsed into
245:     "saved".
246: 
247:     **Deliberately NOT an** :class:`~engine.arrival_head_attestation.AttestationRefusal`.
248:     Every member of that family means the operation did not happen. This one
249:     means the opposite — the data is committed and durable — so a caller
250:     catching the refusal root to mean "nothing changed" must not catch this
251:     too. Propagating past those handlers is the never-silent mechanism, and
252:     the type name is the whole message.
253: 
254:     :attr:`head` is where the lineage now stands. :attr:`commit` is the full
255:     transition for the append and replicate paths, and ``None`` for a mint,
256:     which returns a head rather than a commit.
257:     """
258: 
259:     def __init__(
260:         self, message: str, *, head: Head, commit: Commit | None = None
261:     ) -> None:
262:         super().__init__(message)
263:         self.head = head
264:         self.commit = commit

1664: class AttestedLedger(_HeadObservation):
1665:     """An :class:`~engine.arrival_contract.ArrivalLedger` that remembers.
1666: 
1667:     Two things, and no third: it compares at construction, and it journals
1668:     after a successful ``mint``, ``append`` or ``replicate``. Everything else
1669:     is delegation.
1670: 
1671:     **Delegation is by explicit method, never** ``__getattr__``. A catch-all
1672:     would forward ``import_prefix`` — a mutation the contract Protocol
1673:     deliberately does not declare but which is in ``LEDGER_MUTATIONS``
1674:     regardless, because the custody/reads separation must cover every op that
1675:     can change what a lineage holds. Forwarded silently, it would be a mutation
1676:     reachable through the attested handle that the seam does not journal: a
1677:     hole in the witness, opened by a convenience nobody wrote down. It is
1678:     therefore NOT reachable through this wrapper, and a future op added to the
1679:     adapter is not reachable either until somebody decides how it is witnessed.
1680: 
1681:     **The IndeterminateComparison posture** — the WP's flagged design point — is
1682:     in :meth:`_degraded`, with the argument.
1683:     """
1684: 
1685:     def __init__(
1686:         self,
1687:         ledger: ArrivalLedger,
1688:         *,
1689:         location: str,
1690:         query: ArrivalQuery | None = None,
1691:         clock: Callable[[], float] = time.time,
1692:         binding: BindingIdentity | None = None,
1693:     ) -> None:
1694:         """Compare, then write what the comparison earned.
1695: 
1696:         ``clock`` is injected because WP1's module reads no clock — every
1697:         ``observed_at`` there is a caller-supplied argument — and this is the
1698:         caller. It is what keeps staleness reporting testable without freezing
1699:         time inside the journal.
1700: 
1701:         **This is the ONLY place the open path writes**, and that is the whole
1702:         of "nothing writes until every refusal has had its chance". Observation
1703:         is pure with respect to the journal — it returns an :class:`_Earned`
1704:         and writes nothing — so an open that refuses anywhere leaves no memory
1705:         claiming it accepted the thing it refused.
1706: 
1707:         Stating it per-branch was not enough: the first-contact branch deferred
1708:         while the advance branch wrote inline, and a store that passed the
1709:         journal comparison and then failed the projection one kept an
1710:         ``advance`` entry for the head it had just been refused
1711:         (``finding:s3wp3-gate-advance-journaled-before-projection-refusal``).
1712:         The rule is now carried by the return type, and
1713:         ``test_only_the_constructor_and_the_producers_write_to_the_journal``
1714:         pins it against the branch somebody adds next.
1715:         """
1716:         super().__init__(
1717:             ledger, location=location, query=query, clock=clock, binding=binding
1718:         )
1719:         self._ledger = ledger
1720:         report, earned = self._observe()
1721:         if earned is not None:
1722:             self._write(earned)
1723:         self.opened: OpenReport = report
1724:         """What the compare-on-open established. See :class:`OpenReport`."""
1725: 
1726:     def _write(self, earned: _Earned) -> None:
1727:         """Write what an observation earned, once it has earned it.
1728: 
1729:         A bootstrap goes through :func:`bootstrap` rather than straight to
1730:         ``append_entry`` so that the level validation and the binding stay on
1731:         one path — a seam that appended its own bootstrap would be the second
1732:         place the canonical form is applied, which is how two spellings
1733:         diverge.
1734:         """
1735:         if earned.kind is Kind.BOOTSTRAP:
1736:             bootstrap(
1737:                 earned.head,
1738:                 level=earned.level,
1739:                 location=self._location,
1740:                 observed_at=self._clock(),
1741:                 binding=self._binding,
1742:             )
1743:             return
1744:         append_entry(
1745:             HeadAttestation(
1746:                 head=earned.head,
1747:                 kind=earned.kind,
1748:                 level=earned.level,
1749:                 observed_at=self._clock(),
1750:                 location=self._canonical,
1751:             )
1752:         )
1753: 
1754:     def mint(self, options: Mapping[str, Any]) -> Head:
1755:         """Mint through the wrapper, and the bootstrap receipt is a consequence.
1756: 
1757:         Slice 4's sidecar gets its receipt by using the ordinary
1758:         registry-opened ledger: there is no privileged path and no special API,
1759:         which mirrors the sink's own rule that migration appends through
1760:         ordinary ``append``. The level is ``mint`` — this process minted the
1761:         genesis, so the journal is witnessed from genesis forward and slice 6's
1762:         exit criterion can tell it apart from a store trusted on sight.
1763:         """
1764:         head = self._ledger.mint(options)
1765:         try:
1766:             bootstrap(
1767:                 head,
1768:                 level=Level.MINT,
1769:                 location=self._location,
1770:                 observed_at=self._clock(),
1771:                 binding=self._binding,
1772:             )
1773:         except (OSError, AttestationRefusal) as exc:
1774:             raise NotWitnessed(
1775:                 f"the genesis for lineage {head.lineage} is COMMITTED at "
1776:                 f"{self._canonical}, and its bootstrap receipt could not be "
1777:                 f"written: {exc}. The store exists and this machine has no "
1778:                 "memory of it, so its next open is first contact rather than "
1779:                 "the mint-level receipt slice 6 checks for",
1780:                 head=head,
1781:             ) from exc
1782:         return head
1783: 
1784:     def append(
1785:         self, expected: Head | None, drafts: Sequence[RecordDraft]
1786:     ) -> Commit:
1787:         """Append, then journal the commit — §D.3's O(1)-unchanged-open design.
1788: 
1789:         A successful append returns a ``Commit`` whose ``before`` and ``after``
1790:         are full heads, compared under the full-head fence: the writer already
1791:         holds the strongest descent evidence there is, so recording it at
1792:         ``Level.COMMIT`` means the NEXT open sees the presented head equal to
1793:         the remembered one and takes the O(1) unchanged branch. Without this
1794:         the next open would observe an advance and pay a verified walk for
1795:         evidence this process had already gathered and thrown away.
1796:         """
1797:         commit = self._ledger.append(expected, drafts)
1798:         self._witness(commit)
1799:         return commit
1800: 
1801:     def replicate(
1802:         self, expected: Head | None, records: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]]
1803:     ) -> Commit:
1804:         """Replicate, then journal the commit. Same fence, same evidence.
1805: 
1806:         Replication assigns no coordinates — it inserts an exact
1807:         pre-coordinated suffix — but it goes through the same full-head compare,
1808:         so the commit it returns is the same evidence an append's is and it is
1809:         recorded at the same level.
1810:         """
1811:         commit = self._ledger.replicate(expected, records)
1812:         self._witness(commit)
1813:         return commit
1814: 
1815:     def _witness(self, commit: Commit) -> None:
1816:         """Journal a commit, or say loudly that it went unwitnessed.
1817: 
1818:         The append has already landed when this runs, so a failure here cannot
1819:         be reported as a failed append — the store has advanced past its
1820:         witness, and §05's ``committed`` versus ``witnessed`` distinction is
1821:         exactly the thing that must not be collapsed into "saved". Raising
1822:         :class:`NotWitnessed` is the never-silent mechanism, and its type says
1823:         the data is safe.
1824:         """
1825:         try:
1826:             append_entry(
1827:                 HeadAttestation(
1828:                     head=commit.after,
1829:                     kind=Kind.ADVANCE,
1830:                     level=Level.COMMIT,
1831:                     observed_at=self._clock(),
1832:                     location=self._canonical,
1833:                 )
1834:             )
1835:         except (OSError, AttestationRefusal) as exc:
1836:             raise NotWitnessed(
1837:                 f"{len(commit.records)} record(s) are COMMITTED at "
1838:                 f"{self._canonical} through {_head_text(commit.after)}, and "
1839:                 f"the journal entry for them could not be written: {exc}. The "
1840:                 "store has advanced past its witness, so the next open will "
1841:                 "observe an advance and pay a verified walk for it",
1842:                 head=commit.after,
1843:                 commit=commit,
1844:             ) from exc
1845: 
1846:     # -- reads, delegated unchanged ----------------------------------------
1847: 
1848:     def head(self, lineage: str | None = None) -> Head:
1849:         """The currently complete head."""
1850:         return self._ledger.head(lineage)
1851: 
1852:     def head_at(self, watermark: Watermark) -> Head:
1853:         """Resolve a projection's watermark into the verified head it names."""
1854:         return self._ledger.head_at(watermark)
1855: 
1856:     def read(self, coordinate: int) -> Mapping[str, Any]:
1857:         """The exact record, after establishing its membership in the lineage."""
1858:         return self._ledger.read(coordinate)
1859: 
1860:     def scan(
1861:         self, *, after: int | None = None, through: Head | None = None
1862:     ) -> Any:
1863:         """An ordered, consistent prefix range and no later record."""
1864:         return self._ledger.scan(after=after, through=through)
1865: 
1866:     def verify(self, scope: VerifyScope) -> Head:
1867:         """Verify through a named head; return the head the claim covers."""
1868:         return self._ledger.verify(scope)
1869: 
1870:     def export(self, *, through: Head, codec: str) -> ExportedPrefix:
1871:         """Deterministic portable records plus a manifest, for a prefix."""
1872:         return self._ledger.export(through=through, codec=codec)
1873: 
1874:     def capabilities(self) -> Capabilities:
1875:         """The wrapped backend's self-report, unchanged.
1876: 
1877:         Not this wrapper's. Everything ``Capabilities`` describes — profiles,
1878:         durability, verification levels, concurrency, codecs — is a property of
1879:         the backend holding the bytes, and the seam changes none of them.
1880:         Amending the report to mention the witness would be advertising a
1881:         guarantee at the layer that does not provide it.
1882:         """
1883:         return self._ledger.capabilities()
1884: 
1885:     def close(self) -> None:
1886:         """Release the wrapped custody handle, when the adapter owns one."""
1887:         close = getattr(self._ledger, "close", None)
1888:         if callable(close):
1889:             close()

=== SELECTED DEFINITIONS libs/engine/src/engine/arrival_registry.py ===
121: def descriptor_for(
122:     ast: VertexFile, vertex_path: Path | None = None
123: ) -> StoreDescriptor | None:
124:     """Which adapter opens this vertex's store, or None if no adapter does.
125: 
126:     Two answers, and only the first describes a ledger:
127: 
128:     **Explicit** — the vertex declared ``backend=``. The declared name wins,
129:     and there is NO cross-check against the location's suffix. A
130:     ``backend="file"`` on a ``.duckdb``-named path is legal, because under §02
131:     the suffix carries no meaning once the backend is declared; adding a "do
132:     they agree?" check would re-admit inference through the back door on the
133:     very change that removes it. An unregistered name is not this function's
134:     to refuse — see :meth:`BackendRegistry.open`.
135: 
136:     **No descriptor** — a bare legacy store, an aggregate (``combine``), or a
137:     loops-only vertex has no explicit adapter declaration. Suffix inference
138:     ends here: a ``.arrival`` suffix does not synthesize ``backend="file"``.
139: 
140:     ``lineage`` and ``role`` are declaration claims, not grants. The registry
141:     checks them against the presented genesis and backend capabilities, then
142:     keeps enforcing them on the returned custody handle.
143:     """
144:     if ast.store is None or ast.store_backend is None:
145:         return None
146: 
147:     declaration = ast.store_backend
148:     if declaration.name == FILE_BACKEND:
149:         # This is the built-in file adapter's interpretation of a location:
150:         # relative paths are relative to the declaring vertex, never cwd.
151:         from .residence import canonical_store_path
152: 
153:         location = str(canonical_store_path(ast.store, vertex_path))
154:     else:
155:         # No pathlib round-trip for another adapter. A DSN or service URL is
156:         # opaque, including repeated slashes and query text.
157:         if ast.store_location is None:
158:             raise ValueError(
159:                 f"non-file backend {declaration.name!r} has no opaque store "
160:                 "location; construct it by parsing a descriptor declaration"
161:             )
162:         location = ast.store_location
163: 
164:     role = None if declaration.role is None else Profile(declaration.role)
165:     return StoreDescriptor(
166:         backend=declaration.name,
167:         location=location,
168:         lineage=declaration.lineage,
169:         role=role,
170:     )

173: def _open_file_backend(
174:     descriptor: StoreDescriptor,
175: ) -> tuple[ArrivalLedger, ArrivalQuery]:
176:     """Open the ``.arrival`` log and the projection beside it.
177: 
178:     Imported lazily, inside the opener: ``import engine.arrival_registry``
179:     must not drag sqlite3 and the whole arrival codec behind it for a caller
180:     that only wants to NAME a descriptor. That is the same reason the contract
181:     module is separate from the adapter, held one level up.
182:     """
183:     from .arrival import ArrivalLog
184:     from .arrival_file_backend import FileLedger, FileQuery, file_projection_path
185: 
186:     location = Path(descriptor.location)
187:     return FileLedger(ArrivalLog(location)), FileQuery(file_projection_path(location))

198: def _open_file_maintenance(descriptor: StoreDescriptor) -> ProjectionMaintenance:
199:     """Build the file adapter's derived-state authority lazily."""
200:     from .arrival_file_backend import FileProjectionMaintenance, file_projection_path
201: 
202:     location = Path(descriptor.location)
203:     return FileProjectionMaintenance(location, file_projection_path(location))

365: class BackendRegistry:
366:     """Backend name → opener, and nothing more.
367: 
368:     A lookup table, not a cache: it holds no open handles and opening the
369:     same descriptor twice yields two independent pairs. Custody lifetime
370:     belongs to whoever asked for it.
371:     """
372: 
373:     def __init__(self) -> None:
374:         self._openers: dict[str, BackendOpener] = {}
375:         self._bindings: dict[str, BindingProvider] = {}
376:         self._maintenance_openers: dict[str, MaintenanceOpener] = {}
377:         self._search_maintenance_openers: dict[str, SearchMaintenanceOpener] = {}
378: 
379:     @classmethod
380:     def with_builtin_backends(cls) -> BackendRegistry:
381:         """A registry holding the adapters that ship in this package."""
382:         registry = cls()
383:         registry.register(
384:             FILE_BACKEND,
385:             _open_file_backend,
386:             binding_provider=_file_binding,
387:             maintenance_opener=_open_file_maintenance,
388:             search_maintenance_opener=_open_file_search_maintenance,
389:         )
390:         return registry
391: 
392:     def register(
393:         self,
394:         name: str,
395:         opener: BackendOpener,
396:         *,
397:         binding_provider: BindingProvider | None = None,
398:         maintenance_opener: MaintenanceOpener | None = None,
399:         search_maintenance_opener: SearchMaintenanceOpener | None = None,
400:     ) -> None:
401:         """Bind a backend name to its opener.
402: 
403:         Re-registering a name refuses. A silent overwrite would let import
404:         order decide which adapter answers to "file", and the failure would
405:         surface as a store opening through the wrong codec rather than as an
406:         error at the point of the mistake. ``binding_provider`` and the
407:         private ``maintenance_opener`` are separate metadata so the opener's
408:         tuple remains stable. Binding is checked before the opener runs, and
409:         maintenance remains reachable only through the attested coordinator.
410:         """
411:         if name in self._openers:
412:             raise ValueError(f"backend {name!r} is already registered")
413:         self._openers[name] = opener
414:         self._bindings[name] = (
415:             binding_provider
416:             if binding_provider is not None
417:             else lambda descriptor, backend=name: opaque_binding(
418:                 backend, descriptor.location
419:             )
420:         )
421:         if maintenance_opener is not None:
422:             self._maintenance_openers[name] = maintenance_opener
423:         if search_maintenance_opener is not None:
424:             self._search_maintenance_openers[name] = search_maintenance_opener
425: 
426:     def registered(self) -> tuple[str, ...]:
427:         """The backend names this registry answers to, sorted."""
428:         return tuple(sorted(self._openers))
429: 
430:     def _maintenance_for(self, descriptor: StoreDescriptor) -> ProjectionMaintenance:
431:         """Private adapter factory used only after an attested registry open.
432: 
433:         The public maintenance entry point is
434:         :func:`engine.arrival_maintenance.sync_projection`. Keeping this
435:         factory private prevents a raw derived-state authority from becoming a
436:         second registry open path that omits head attestation.
437:         """
438:         opener = self._maintenance_openers.get(descriptor.backend)
439:         if opener is None:
440:             raise NotSupported(
441:                 f"backend {descriptor.backend!r} does not provide projection maintenance"
442:             )
443:         return opener(descriptor)
444: 
445:     def _search_maintenance_for(self, descriptor: StoreDescriptor) -> SearchIndexMaintenance:
446:         """Private FTS provider reached only by the attested coordinator."""
447:         opener = self._search_maintenance_openers.get(descriptor.backend)
448:         if opener is None:
449:             raise NotSupported(
450:                 f"backend {descriptor.backend!r} does not provide search maintenance"
451:             )
452:         return opener(descriptor)
453: 
454:     def open(
455:         self, descriptor: StoreDescriptor
456:     ) -> tuple[ArrivalLedger, ArrivalQuery]:
457:         """The custody half and the reads half for this descriptor.
458: 
459:         Refuses an unregistered name with
460:         :class:`~engine.arrival_contract.UnknownBackend`, naming what IS
461:         registered. Refusing is the whole point: §02 forbids inferring a
462:         backend from a suffix, so an unregistered name has no fallback to
463:         degrade into — and a registry that guessed would reintroduce
464:         inference at the one seam built to remove it.
465: 
466:         **The custody half comes back wrapped, always, with no way to ask for
467:         it unwrapped** (slice 3 §D.1). :class:`~engine.arrival_head_seam.AttestedLedger`
468:         compares the head this store presents against the one this machine last
469:         accepted, and journals every commit made through it. A configuration
470:         switch for a safety property is a shape the arrival vocabulary
471:         ratchet's own denylist rejects: custody is structural rather than
472:         configured, so there is no flag here and no second opener that skips
473:         the comparison.
474: 
475:         The comparison happens at construction, which means **this method
476:         raises what the comparison raises**: a rollback, a fork, a rewrite, a
477:         replacement or a lost store refuses the open rather than being reported
478:         afterwards by a caller who might not ask. This is also why the seam
479:         sits here rather than deeper — the opener above performs no recovery,
480:         so the comparison sees the store as it is rather than as a catch-up
481:         just repaired it.
482:         """
483:         ledger, query, binding = self._open_components(descriptor)
484:         try:
485:             attested = AttestedLedger(
486:                 ledger, location=descriptor.location, query=query, binding=binding
487:             )
488:         except BaseException:
489:             _close_handle(query)
490:             _close_handle(ledger)
491:             raise
492:         return attested, query
493: 
494: 
495:     def _open_components(
496:         self, descriptor: StoreDescriptor
497:     ) -> tuple[ArrivalLedger, ArrivalQuery, BindingIdentity]:
498:         """Private resources for attested open or the bounded restore procedure.
499: 
500:         Never returned to consumers. Both callers own comparison, witnessing,
501:         and closure; restoration exposes only its completed receipt.
502:         """
503:         opener = self._openers.get(descriptor.backend)
504:         if opener is None:
505:             known = ", ".join(self.registered()) or "(none)"
506:             raise UnknownBackend(
507:                 f"no adapter registered for backend {descriptor.backend!r} "
508:                 f"(registered: {known})"
509:             )
510:         provider = self._bindings[descriptor.backend]
511:         try:
512:             binding = provider(descriptor)
513:         except BindingProbeError as exc:
514:             raise BindingProbeUnanswered(
515:                 f"{descriptor.location} exists and storage would not establish "
516:                 f"its file identity ({exc}); nothing has been accepted or "
517:                 "written — repair the location or retry"
518:             ) from exc
519:         _validate_binding(descriptor, binding)
520:         ledger, query = opener(descriptor)
521:         try:
522:             capabilities = ledger.capabilities()
523:             if (
524:                 descriptor.role is not None
525:                 and descriptor.role not in capabilities.profiles
526:             ):
527:                 offered = ", ".join(
528:                     sorted(profile.value for profile in capabilities.profiles)
529:                 ) or "(none)"
530:                 raise NotSupported(
531:                     f"backend {descriptor.backend!r} cannot open location "
532:                     f"{descriptor.location!r} as {descriptor.role.value}: "
533:                     f"capabilities advertise {offered}"
534:                 )
535: 
536:             # Check a presented genesis before AttestedLedger can turn a
537:             # first-contact head into a bootstrap journal entry. An absent or
538:             # not-yet-minted ledger has no genesis to compare; its later mint
539:             # remains pinned by _DescriptorLedger.
540:             if descriptor.lineage is not None:
541:                 try:
542:                     presented = ledger.verify(Open())
543:                 except Exception:  # backend-specific absence/corruption
544:                     presented = None
545:                 if (
546:                     presented is not None
547:                     and presented.lineage != descriptor.lineage
548:                 ):
549:                     raise NotAuthority(
550:                         f"descriptor names lineage {descriptor.lineage}, but "
551:                         f"the genesis at {descriptor.location!r} opens "
552:                         f"{presented.lineage}; the declaration is a check, "
553:                         "not a replacement for genesis"
554:                     )
555: 
556:             return _DescriptorLedger(ledger, descriptor), query, binding
557:         except BaseException:
558:             _close_handle(query)
559:             _close_handle(ledger)
560:             raise
561: 
562:     def restore_forward(
563:         self,
564:         source: StoreDescriptor,
565:         receiver: StoreDescriptor,
566:         *,
567:         through: Head | None = None,
568:     ) -> RestoreForwardResult:
569:         """Restore an existing exact prefix without granting an open handle."""
570:         from .arrival_restore import _restore_forward
571: 
572:         return _restore_forward(self, source, receiver, through=through)

369: def _commit_dict(commit: Commit | None) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
370:     """Serialize commit identity without copying backend record bodies."""

372:         return None

=== SELECTED DEFINITIONS libs/engine/src/engine/declaration.py ===
221: def resolve_declaration_documents_from_snapshot(
222:     anchor: DeclarationAnchor, facts: list[Fact] | tuple[Fact, ...]
223: ) -> list[dict[str, Any]] | None | Unhistorized:
224:     """Fold declaration documents from one already-bounded query snapshot.
225: 
226:     This is the pure sibling of :func:`resolve_declaration_documents`. The
227:     caller supplies internal facts through its query prefix and the exact
228:     store-local identity anchor observed on that same transaction. It therefore
229:     never opens SQLite, does not consult a vertex file, and never guesses which
230:     ``_decl.genesis`` is self from rows that may have arrived by merge.
231:     """
232:     internal = sorted(
233:         (fact for fact in facts if is_internal_kind(fact.kind)),
234:         key=lambda fact: (fact.arrival_ordinal, fact.arrival_seq),
235:     )
236:     if anchor.own_lineage is None:
237:         if any(fact.kind == DECL_GENESIS for fact in internal):
238:             raise UnadoptedLineage(
239:                 "declaration genesis rows occur in this snapshot without an "
240:                 "own_lineage identity marker; facts cannot identify self"
241:             )
242:         return None
243:     genesis = anchor.genesis
244:     if genesis is None or genesis.id != anchor.own_lineage or genesis.kind != DECL_GENESIS:
245:         raise DeclarationResolutionError(
246:             f"own_lineage marker {anchor.own_lineage!r} has no matching "
247:             "_decl.genesis row in this snapshot"
248:         )
249:     payload = genesis.payload
250:     protocol = payload.get("protocol", 1)
251:     if protocol > DECLARATION_PROTOCOL_VERSION:
252:         raise UnsupportedProtocol(
253:             f"genesis protocol {protocol} exceeds supported {DECLARATION_PROTOCOL_VERSION}"
254:         )
255:     if genesis.arrival_ordinal > max((fact.arrival_ordinal for fact in internal), default=-1):
256:         return Unhistorized(list(payload.get("documents", ())))
257: 
258:     docs: dict[tuple[str, str], dict[str, Any]] = {}
259:     for document in payload.get("documents", ()):
260:         docs[(document["kind"], document["subject"])] = document
261:     for fact in internal:
262:         if fact.kind == DECL_GENESIS:
263:             continue
264:         row_payload = fact.payload
265:         if row_payload.get("lineage") != anchor.own_lineage:
266:             continue
267:         if fact.kind in _TOMBSTONE_OF_DEFINED:
268:             docs.pop((_TOMBSTONE_OF_DEFINED[fact.kind], row_payload.get("subject")), None)
269:         elif fact.kind in _DEFINED_KINDS:
270:             subject = row_payload.get("subject")
271:             docs[(fact.kind, subject)] = {
272:                 "kind": fact.kind,
273:                 "subject": subject,
274:                 "payload": row_payload.get("payload", {}),
275:             }
276:     return list(docs.values())

852: def validate_arrival_runtime_identity(
853:     vertex_name: str,
854:     loop_names: Iterable[str],
855:     *,
856:     refusal: Callable[[str], BaseException],
857: ) -> None:
858:     """Refuse loop identities ambiguous under the current Arrival tick profile.
859: 
860:     Arrival runtime interpretation reserves the vertex name from every local
861:     loop name. ``cite`` is included because materialization installs that
862:     implicit loop even when it is absent from the declaration. Callers supply
863:     their operation-specific pre-mutation refusal family; evidence-only
864:     declaration reconstruction deliberately does not invoke this validator.
865:     """
866:     if vertex_name == "cite" or vertex_name in loop_names:
867:         raise refusal(
868:             f"Arrival runtime reserves vertex name {vertex_name!r} "
869:             "from the loop-name namespace"
870:         )
